家长对 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 的认识及其预防:沙特阿拉伯利雅得的一项横断面研究

Parents’ awareness of respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) and its prevention: a cross-sectional study in Riyadh, Saudi Arabia
作者： Ahmed J. Alzahrani, Sara Aloliyan, Deem Albakran, Aljohara Alhoshan, Rama Alaklabi, Sarah Aljalajel, Tala Alfraih
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
This study focused on respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) as a primary cause of lower respiratory tract infections in infants and young children globally, with an estimated 33 million cases and 100,000 deaths annually in children under five. The central question was to assess the prevalence and determinants of parental awareness regarding RSV and its prevention among parents in Riyadh, Saudi Arabia.
证据基础与方法
2026 年 2 月至 3 月期间,对居住在沙特阿拉伯利雅得的父母进行了一项基于社区的横断面调查。通过在线平台进行便利抽样,招募了至少有一个孩子的父母。一份结构化、自我管理的阿拉伯语问卷评估了人口特征、既往 RSV 认知、传播 传播途径和临床表现的知识、高危人群的识别以及预防性免疫的认知。使用描述性统计和卡方检验对数据进行分析。
共识与结论
研究显示,利雅得家长对 RSV 的认知度极低,只有 35.0%(95% CI:28.7-41.8%)以前听说过 RSV。多个领域的知识匮乏,相当大比例的人无法识别 传播 机制、症状或高危人群。大多数人不知道预防性注射的存在。在了解 RSV 预防的人群中,社交媒体是主要的信息来源,而医疗保健专业人员只占少数。
争议与缺口
该研究发现 RSV 流行病学 和预防的所有领域都存在重大知识差距。教育水平与 RSV 认识呈正相关但不显着,这表明仅靠高等教育可能不足以提高 RSV 认识。该研究没有探讨获取医疗保健信息的潜在障碍或不同教育干预措施的有效性。
科研与实践启示
这些发现强调了公共卫生迫切需要针对社区教育和加强医疗保健系统的多层次干预措施。将 RSV 教育纳入母婴健康服务并制定适合文化的健康传播策略对于减轻沙特阿拉伯 RSV 相关发病率和 死亡率 的负担至关重要。

Parents’ awareness of respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) and its prevention: a cross-sectional study in Riyadh, Saudi Arabia

Authors: Ahmed J. Alzahrani, Sara Aloliyan, Deem Albakran, Aljohara Alhoshan, Rama Alaklabi, Sarah Aljalajel, Tala Alfraih
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) represents a major public health challenge as a primary cause of lower respiratory tract infections in infants and young children globally, with an estimated 33 million cases and 100,000 deaths annually in children under five. Despite recent advances in RSV immunoprophylaxis, parental understanding of RSV epidemiology, transmission dynamics, vulnerable populations, and available preventive strategies remains inadequately characterized in Middle Eastern populations. This study aimed to assess the prevalence and determinants of parental awareness regarding RSV and its prevention among parents in Riyadh, Saudi Arabia. We conducted a community-based cross-sectional survey among parents residing in Riyadh, Saudi Arabia, between February and March 2026. Using convenience sampling through online platforms, we recruited parents with at least one child. A structured, self-administered Arabic questionnaire assessed demographic characteristics, prior RSV awareness, knowledge of transmission routes and clinical manifestations, recognition of high-risk groups, and awareness of preventive immunization. Data were analyzed using descriptive statistics and chi-square tests to examine associations between demographic variables and RSV awareness. Among 214 eligible parents, the majority were female and held bachelor’s degrees. Only 35.0% (95% CI: 28.7–41.8%) had previously heard of RSV. Knowledge deficits were substantial across multiple domains, with considerable proportions unable to identify transmission mechanisms, symptoms, or high-risk populations. The majority were unaware that preventive injections exist. Among those aware of RSV prevention, social media was the predominant information source, while healthcare professionals accounted for a small minority. Educational level showed a positive but non-significant association with RSV awareness. This study reveals critically low prevalence of RSV awareness among parents in Riyadh, with substantial knowledge gaps across all domains of RSV epidemiology and prevention. These findings underscore an urgent public health need for multi-level interventions targeting both community education and healthcare system strengthening. Integrating RSV education into maternal-child health services and developing culturally appropriate health communication strategies are essential to reduce the burden of RSV-related morbidity and mortality in Saudi Arabia.

Scope and question
This study focused on respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) as a primary cause of lower respiratory tract infections in infants and young children globally, with an estimated 33 million cases and 100,000 deaths annually in children under five. The central question was to assess the prevalence and determinants of parental awareness regarding RSV and its prevention among parents in Riyadh, Saudi Arabia.
Evidence base and method
A community-based cross-sectional survey was conducted among parents residing in Riyadh, Saudi Arabia, between February and March 2026. Using convenience sampling through online platforms, parents with at least one child were recruited. A structured, self-administered Arabic questionnaire assessed demographic characteristics, prior RSV awareness, knowledge of transmission routes and clinical manifestations, recognition of high-risk groups, and awareness of preventive immunization. Data were analyzed using descriptive statistics and chi-square tests.
Consensus and conclusions
The study revealed a critically low prevalence of RSV awareness among parents in Riyadh, with only 35.0% (95% CI: 28.7–41.8%) having previously heard of RSV. Knowledge deficits were substantial across multiple domains, with considerable proportions unable to identify transmission mechanisms, symptoms, or high-risk populations. The majority were unaware that preventive injections exist. Among those aware of RSV prevention, social media was the predominant information source, while healthcare professionals accounted for a small minority.
Controversies and gaps
The study identified substantial knowledge gaps across all domains of RSV epidemiology and prevention. Educational level showed a positive but non-significant association with RSV awareness, suggesting that higher education alone may not sufficiently improve RSV awareness. The study did not explore potential barriers to accessing healthcare information or the effectiveness of different educational interventions.
Research and practice implications
These findings underscore an urgent public health need for multi-level interventions targeting both community education and healthcare system strengthening. Integrating RSV education into maternal-child health services and developing culturally appropriate health communication strategies are essential to reduce the burden of RSV-related morbidity and mortality in Saudi Arabia.

呼吸道合胞病毒 预防:母体 呼吸道合胞病毒 还是长效单克隆抗体?对现状的简要叙述回顾

Respiratory Syncytial Virus Prophylaxis: Maternal Vaccination or Long-Acting Monoclonal Antibodies? A Brief Narrative Review of Current Status
作者： Fani Ladomenou, Ioanna-Andriana Psarra, Despoina Gkentzi
摘要中文翻译呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 仍然是婴儿早期严重下呼吸道感染的主要原因,最高负担集中在生命的最初几个月。两种免疫预防策略——母体 RSVpreF 疫苗接种 和长效单克隆抗体——现在提供有效的早期生命保护,但在生物学机制、操作要求和规划意义方面有所不同。母体 疫苗接种 诱导经胎盘转移的高质量中和抗体,从出生起立即提供保护,并有效融入产前护理平台。然而,其有效性受到妊娠时间、生理抗体减弱以及分娩前不久出现 疫苗接种 时保护作用减弱的限制。长效单克隆抗体,包括 尼塞韦单抗 和 clesrovimab,可绕过母体免疫变异性,并提供跨胎龄(包括早产儿)的标准化、全季保护。经济评估表明,这两种策略都具有成本效益,尽管它们的相对价值取决于当地的 流行病学、定价和覆盖模式。单克隆抗体和母体 疫苗接种 的经济成果高度依赖于具体情况,并受到区域 RSV 流行病学、医疗保健利用率、产品定价和实施能力的影响,而不是普遍偏向一种策略而不是另一种策略。全球公平考虑仍然至关重要:产前保健机会的差异、供应链的脆弱性和价格差异可能会扩大高收入国家和低收入国家之间的差距。剩余的证据差距——包括长期安全监测、保护的持久性、病毒 进化 和实施挑战——强调需要继续研究并将这些工具仔细整合到国家免疫规划中。

母体 疫苗接种 诱导经胎盘转移的高质量中和抗体,从出生起立即提供保护,并有效融入产前护理平台。

剩余的证据差距——包括长期安全监测、保护的持久性、病毒 进化 和实施挑战——强调需要继续研究并将这些工具仔细整合到国家免疫规划中。

Respiratory Syncytial Virus Prophylaxis: Maternal Vaccination or Long-Acting Monoclonal Antibodies? A Brief Narrative Review of Current Status

Authors: Fani Ladomenou, Ioanna-Andriana Psarra, Despoina Gkentzi
Original abstract (English)
Respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) remains a leading cause of severe lower respiratory tract infection in early infancy, with the highest burden concentrated in the first months of life. Two immunoprophylactic strategies—maternal RSVpreF vaccination and long-acting monoclonal antibodies—now offer effective early life protection, yet differ in biological mechanisms, operational requirements, and programmatic implications. Maternal vaccination induces high-quality neutralizing antibodies that are transferred transplacentally, providing immediate protection from birth and integrating efficiently into antenatal care platforms. However, its effectiveness is constrained by gestational timing, physiological antibody waning, and reduced protection when vaccination occurs shortly before delivery. Long-acting monoclonal antibodies, including nirsevimab and clesrovimab, bypass maternal immune variability and provide standardized, season-long protection across gestational ages, including preterm infants. Economic evaluations demonstrate that both strategies can be cost-effective, though their relative value depends on local epidemiology, pricing, and coverage patterns. Economic outcomes for monoclonal antibodies and maternal vaccination are highly context-dependent and influenced by regional RSV epidemiology, healthcare utilization, product pricing, and implementation capacity, rather than universally favoring one strategy over the other. Global equity considerations remain critical: disparities in antenatal care access, supply chain fragility, and pricing differentials may widen gaps between high- and low-income countries. Remaining evidence gaps—including long-term safety monitoring, durability of protection, viral evolution, and implementation challenges—underscore the need for continued research and careful integration of these tools into national immunization programs.
View review five-element analysis

Respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) remains a leading cause of severe lower respiratory tract infection in early infancy, with the highest burden concentrated in the first months of life.
Evidence base and method

Consensus and conclusions
Maternal vaccination induces high-quality neutralizing antibodies that are transferred transplacentally, providing immediate protection from birth and integrating efficiently into antenatal care platforms.
Controversies and gaps
However, its effectiveness is constrained by gestational timing, physiological antibody waning, and reduced protection when vaccination occurs shortly before delivery.
Research and practice implications
Remaining evidence gaps—including long-term safety monitoring, durability of protection, viral evolution, and implementation challenges—underscore the need for continued research and careful integration of these tools into national immunization programs.

呼吸道合胞病毒 prefusion F (RSVpreF) 母体 疫苗 用于哥伦比亚婴儿疾病预防的成本效益

Cost-utility of respiratory syncytial virus prefusion F (RSVpreF) maternal vaccine for disease prevention among colombian infants
作者： Jaime Ordóñez, Beatriz Ospina, Jordi Ordóñez‐Llanos
摘要中文翻译摘要 背景 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 是哥伦比亚急性下呼吸道感染的主要原因之一,因此,由于不存在广泛的 呼吸道合胞病毒,使用 RSV 预融合 F 蛋白 疫苗 (RSVpreF) 进行母体免疫是一种有前途的策略。本研究旨在评估 RSVpreF 孕产妇 疫苗 在预防哥伦比亚婴儿 RSV 感染方面的成本效益。方法 我们使用马尔可夫模型模拟哥伦比亚出生 队列研究 的一生,比较母亲 RSVpreF 疫苗接种 与无干预的情况,反映哥伦比亚当前的护理标准。该模型纳入了与 RSV 相关的门诊就诊、住院和死亡情况。我们从临床试验、同行评审文献和哥伦比亚国家健康数据库中收集了数据。从哥伦比亚医疗保健系统的角度分析成本,调整为 2024 美元,并对成本和结果应用 5% 的折扣率。我们使用的支付意愿 (WTP) 门槛为每个质量调整生命年 (QALY) 7,491 美元。进行敏感性分析,包括单向敏感性分析和概率敏感性分析,以评估结果的稳健性。结果 母亲 RSVpreF 疫苗接种 与 RSV 相关病例减少约 25,781 例以及 QALY 增加 2,315 例相关。增量成本效益比 (ICER) 为每 QALY 2,322 美元,远低于 WTP 阈值。敏感性分析确定 疫苗 有效性、疫苗 成本和 RSV 住院率是成本效益的主要驱动因素。概率敏感性分析表明,在既定的 WTP 阈值下,干预措施具有成本效益的概率为 83.5%。结论 在哥伦比亚,孕产妇 RSVpreF 疫苗接种 是一种具有成本效益的干预措施,可显着降低 RSV 相关发病率和 死亡率,并减轻医疗保健系统的经济负担。研究结果支持将孕产妇 RSV 疫苗接种 纳入国家免疫规划,促进服务欠缺社区的健康公平。临床试验编号 不适用。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本研究评估了母体 RSVprefusion F 蛋白 疫苗 (RSVpreF) 用于预防哥伦比亚婴儿 RSV 感染的成本效用,重点关注 RSV 作为哥伦比亚急性下呼吸道感染的主要原因。
证据基础与方法
马尔可夫模型模拟了哥伦比亚出生的 队列研究 在一生中的情况,比较了没有干预的母亲 RSVpreF 疫苗接种。数据来自临床试验、同行评审文献和哥伦比亚国家健康数据库。从哥伦比亚医疗保健系统的角度分析成本,调整为 2024 美元,并对成本和结果应用 5% 的折扣率。每个 QALY 的支付意愿门槛为 7,491 美元。
共识与结论
母亲 RSVpreF 疫苗接种 与 RSV 相关病例减少约 25,781 例以及 QALY 增加 2,315 例相关。增量成本效益比 (ICER) 为每 QALY 2,322 美元,远低于 WTP 阈值。概率敏感性分析表明,在既定的 WTP 阈值下,干预措施具有成本效益的概率为 83.5%。
争议与缺口
敏感性分析确定 疫苗 有效性、疫苗 成本和 RSV 住院率是成本效益的主要驱动因素。除了这些敏感因素之外,该研究没有报告任何具体的争议或证据差距。
科研与实践启示
研究结果支持将孕产妇 RSV 疫苗接种 纳入国家免疫规划,促进服务欠缺社区的健康公平。The study highlights the significant reductions in RSV-related morbidity,死亡率,and economic burden on the healthcare system achievable through this intervention.

Cost-utility of respiratory syncytial virus prefusion F (RSVpreF) maternal vaccine for disease prevention among colombian infants

Authors: Jaime Ordóñez, Beatriz Ospina, Jordi Ordóñez‐Llanos
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Background Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) is one of the leading causes of acute lower respiratory tract infections in Colombia, therefore, as no widespread antiviral exists, maternal immunization using RSV prefusion F protein vaccine (RSVpreF) represents a promising strategy. This study aims to evaluate the cost-utility of the RSVpreF maternal vaccine in preventing RSV infections among Colombian infants. Methods We used a Markov model to simulate a Colombian birth cohort over a lifetime horizon, comparing maternal RSVpreF vaccination with no intervention, reflecting the current standard of care in Colombia. The model incorporated RSV-related outpatient visits, hospitalizations, and deaths. We collected data from clinical trials, peer-reviewed literature, and Colombian national health databases. Costs were analyzed from the perspective of the Colombian healthcare system, adjusted to 2024 USD, and we applied a 5% discount rate to both costs and outcomes. We used a willingness-to-pay (WTP) threshold of $ 7,491 per quality-adjusted life year (QALY). Sensitivity analyses, including one-way and probabilistic sensitivity analyses, were performed to evaluate the robustness of the results. Results Maternal RSVpreF vaccination was associated with a reduction of approximately 25,781 RSV-related cases and an increase of 2,315 QALYs. The incremental cost-effectiveness ratio (ICER) was $ 2,322 per QALY, well below the WTP threshold. Sensitivity analyses identified vaccine effectiveness, vaccine cost and RSV hospitalization rates as the primary drivers of cost-effectiveness. The probabilistic sensitivity analysis indicated an 83.5% probability that the intervention would be cost-effective at the established WTP threshold. Conclusion Maternal RSVpreF vaccination is a cost-effective intervention in Colombia, resulting in significant reductions in RSV-related morbidity and mortality, as well as in the economic burden on the healthcare system. The findings support the inclusion of maternal RSV vaccination in national immunization programs, promoting health equity in underserved communities. Clinical trial number Not applicable.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This study evaluates the cost-utility of maternal RSVprefusion F protein vaccine (RSVpreF) for preventing RSV infections among Colombian infants, focusing on RSV as a leading cause of acute lower respiratory tract infections in Colombia.
Evidence base and method
A Markov model simulated a Colombian birth cohort over a lifetime horizon, comparing maternal RSVpreF vaccination with no intervention. Data were collected from clinical trials, peer-reviewed literature, and Colombian national health databases. Costs were analyzed from the Colombian healthcare system perspective, adjusted to 2024 USD, with a 5% discount rate applied to costs and outcomes. A willingness-to-pay threshold of $7,491 per QALY was used.
Consensus and conclusions
Maternal RSVpreF vaccination was associated with a reduction of approximately 25,781 RSV-related cases and an increase of 2,315 QALYs. The incremental cost-effectiveness ratio (ICER) was $2,322 per QALY, well below the WTP threshold. The probabilistic sensitivity analysis indicated an 83.5% probability that the intervention would be cost-effective at the established WTP threshold.
Controversies and gaps
Sensitivity analyses identified vaccine effectiveness, vaccine cost, and RSV hospitalization rates as the primary drivers of cost-effectiveness. The study did not report any specific controversies or evidence gaps beyond these sensitivity factors.
Research and practice implications
The findings support the inclusion of maternal RSV vaccination in national immunization programs, promoting health equity in underserved communities. The study highlights the significant reductions in RSV-related morbidity, mortality, and economic burden on the healthcare system achievable through this intervention.

深入了解针对呼吸道病毒的单克隆抗体和 疫苗

Insights Into monoclonal antibodies and vaccines targeting respiratory viruses
作者： Antonella Gambadauro, Giovanna A. Piedimonte, Sara Manti
摘要中文翻译引言 病毒性呼吸道感染是全球儿童发病率和死亡率的主要原因,其中下呼吸道感染(LRTIs)是导致住院和医疗利用的主要因素。在呼吸道病原体中,呼吸道合胞病毒(RSV)是导致生命早期严重疾病的主要原因,并推动了预防策略的重大进展。越来越多的证据也表明,生命早期的病毒感染可能影响长期呼吸道结局,包括复发性喘息和哮喘。研究目的 本文全面概述了儿童病毒感染的流行病学负担、风险因素和长期后果。它还以进化为例研究了预防策略的发展,并探讨了可以从RSV管理中转化为其他主要呼吸道病毒的知识,包括流感、SARS-CoV-2、鼻病毒、人类偏肺病毒(HMPV)和副流感病毒。讨论 RSV通过开发单克隆抗体和母体疫苗接种策略,改变了儿科呼吸道预防,使预防方法从高风险人群转向更广泛的人群保护。结构病毒学的进展使得能够靶向保守的病毒表位,从而提高保护效果和持续时间。类似策略正在探索用于其他呼吸道病毒。然而,抗原变异性、有限的儿科数据和不完整的疫苗有效性仍然是重大障碍。结论 RSV的经验为预防病毒性呼吸道感染提供了坚实的基础。整合被动和主动免疫策略、靶向保守的病毒结构以及改善全球获取途径对于减少儿童呼吸道感染的疾病负担至关重要。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述重点关注 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 作为儿童严重下呼吸道感染的主要原因,检查其流行病学负担、危险因素、长期后果和预防策略,旨在将 RSV 管理见解转化为其他呼吸道病毒。
证据基础与方法
该综述对儿科病毒感染进行了全面概述,使用 RSV 作为案例研究来分析预防策略,但没有具体说明数据库、检索日期、资格标准或研究计数。
共识与结论
RSV通过单克隆抗体和母体疫苗接种推动了预防策略的重大进步,从基于高风险的保护转向了人群水平的保护;针对保守的病毒表位可提高保护的功效和持续时间。
争议与缺口
抗原变异性、有限的儿科数据和不完整的 疫苗 有效性仍然是预防病毒性呼吸道感染的重大障碍。
科研与实践启示
整合被动和主动免疫策略、针对保守的病毒结构以及改善全球可及性对于减轻儿科呼吸道感染的疾病负担至关重要。

Insights Into monoclonal antibodies and vaccines targeting respiratory viruses

Authors: Antonella Gambadauro, Giovanna A. Piedimonte, Sara Manti
Original abstract (English)
Introduction Viral respiratory infections are a leading cause of morbidity and mortality in children worldwide, with lower respiratory tract infections (LRTIs) a major contributor to hospitalizations and healthcare utilization. Among respiratory pathogens, respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) is the leading cause of severe disease in early life and has driven major advances in preventive strategies. Increasing evidence also suggests that early-life viral infections may influence long-term respiratory outcomes, including recurrent wheezing and asthma. Aim of the study This article provides a comprehensive overview of the epidemiological burden, risk factors, and long-term consequences of pediatric viral infections. It also examines RSV as a case study in the evolution of preventive strategies and explores knowledge that can be translated from RSV management to other major respiratory viruses, including influenza, SARS-CoV-2, rhinovirus, human metapneumovirus (HMPV), and parainfluenza. Discussion RSV has reshaped pediatric respiratory prevention through the development of monoclonal antibodies and maternal vaccination strategies, enabling a shift from high-risk-based approaches to broader population-level protection. Advances in structural virology have enabled targeting of conserved viral epitopes, thereby improving efficacy and the duration of protection. Similar strategies are being explored for other respiratory viruses. However, antigenic variability, limited pediatric data, and incomplete vaccine effectiveness remain significant barriers. Conclusion RSV experience provides a robust foundation for preventing viral respiratory infections. Integrating passive and active immunization strategies, targeting conserved viral structures, and improving global access are essential to reduce the disease burden of pediatric respiratory infections.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) as a leading cause of severe lower respiratory tract infections in children, examining its epidemiological burden, risk factors, long-term consequences, and preventive strategies, with the aim of translating RSV management insights to other respiratory viruses.
Evidence base and method
The review provides a comprehensive overview of pediatric viral infections, using RSV as a case study to analyze preventive strategies, but does not specify databases, search dates, eligibility criteria, or study counts.
Consensus and conclusions
RSV has driven major advances in preventive strategies through monoclonal antibodies and maternal vaccination, shifting from high-risk-based to population-level protection; targeting conserved viral epitopes improves efficacy and duration of protection.
Controversies and gaps
Antigenic variability, limited pediatric data, and incomplete vaccine effectiveness remain significant barriers to preventing viral respiratory infections.
Research and practice implications
Integrating passive and active immunization strategies, targeting conserved viral structures, and improving global access are essential to reduce the disease burden of pediatric respiratory infections.

针对免疫功能低下儿童和家庭接触者的流感、COVID-19 和 RSV 疫苗接种。

Influenza, COVID-19, and RSV Vaccinations for Immunocompromised Children and Household Contacts.
作者： Carol M Kao, Hannah Bahakel, Taylor A Heald-Sargent, Timothy D Minniear, Victoria A Statler, Sanya J Thomas, Kathryn E Weakley, Qing Cao, Monica I Ardura, Lara Danziger-Isakov
摘要中文翻译免疫功能低下的儿科患者,包括造血细胞和实体器官移植受者、接受恶性肿瘤化疗的患者以及接受针对自身免疫或炎症状况的生物反应调节剂的患者,有因 疫苗 可预防的呼吸道病毒感染(如流感、SARS-CoV-2 (COVID-19) 和 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV))而患上严重疾病的风险。这些儿童面临更高的住院率、重症监护率和 死亡率 率,反映出免疫系统不成熟、免疫记忆缺失和/或环境暴露增加的影响。由于缺乏免疫反应和 疫苗 在免疫功能低下儿童中的疗效的主要数据,针对免疫功能低下儿童的免疫建议很大程度上是从健康儿童和/或免疫功能低下成人中进行的研究推断出来的。普通人群对 疫苗 犹豫的趋势、社区呼吸道病毒感染持续存在的 传播 以及免疫功能低下儿童及其家庭接触者中 疫苗接种 率不理想的情况进一步加剧了这一弱势群体的风险。随着患有免疫功能低下的儿童数量的增加,护理免疫功能低下儿童的初级保健从业者和专科医生必须确保为这些患者及其家庭和社区接触者提供适当的免疫接种。我们将审查支持当前关于 疫苗 剂量、时间安排和配方的指南的可用数据,以预防免疫功能低下儿童的流感、COVID-19 和 RSV 感染。此外,我们强调了关键知识差距和当前研究领域,旨在提高呼吸道病毒 疫苗 免疫原性和优化保护。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述重点关注 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 以及流感和 SARS-CoV-2,针对免疫功能低下的儿科患者(例如移植接受者、化疗接受者和使用生物调节剂的患者)及其家庭接触者。核心问题涉及 疫苗 的建议、剂量、时间安排和配方,以预防这一弱势群体的严重 RSV 感染。
证据基础与方法
该综述是对现有数据的叙述性综合,因为没有描述系统搜索、数据库或荟萃分析方法。由于免疫功能低下儿童的原始数据有限,它主要根据健康儿童和免疫功能低下成人的研究推断出指南。
共识与结论
由于免疫功能障碍和环境暴露,免疫功能低下的儿童因 RSV 住院、入住 ICU 和 死亡率 的比例更高。当前的 疫苗 建议基于推断证据,并就优化保护的必要性达成共识。
争议与缺口
主要差距包括免疫反应和 疫苗 尤其是在免疫功能低下儿童中的疗效的主要数据不足。次优的 疫苗接种 率和 疫苗 的犹豫使风险缓解进一步复杂化。
科研与实践启示
优先事项包括生成针对特定目标的疗效数据、提高 疫苗接种 在免疫功能低下儿童和接触者中的覆盖率,以及增强 监测 在该人群中针对 RSV 的检测。临床医生必须确保及时免疫接种。

Influenza, COVID-19, and RSV Vaccinations for Immunocompromised Children and Household Contacts.

Authors: Carol M Kao, Hannah Bahakel, Taylor A Heald-Sargent, Timothy D Minniear, Victoria A Statler, Sanya J Thomas, Kathryn E Weakley, Qing Cao, Monica I Ardura, Lara Danziger-Isakov
Original abstract (English)
Immunocompromised pediatric patients, including hematopoietic cell and solid organ transplant recipients, those undergoing chemotherapy for malignancy, and those receiving biologic response modifiers for autoimmune or inflammatory conditions, are at risk for severe disease from vaccine-preventable respiratory viral infections such as influenza, SARS-CoV-2 (COVID-19), and respiratory syncytial virus (RSV). These children face higher rates of hospitalization, intensive care admissions, and mortality, reflecting contributions from an immature immune system, absence of immunologic memory, and/or increased environmental exposures. Immunization recommendations for immunocompromised children are largely extrapolated from studies conducted among healthy children and/or immunocompromised adults due to a paucity of primary data on immunological responses and vaccine efficacy in immunocompromised children. Trends in vaccine hesitancy in the general population, ongoing transmission of respiratory viral infections in the community, and suboptimal vaccination rates among immunocompromised children and their household contacts further compound the risk for this vulnerable population. As the number of children with immunocompromising conditions expands, it is imperative that primary care practitioners and subspecialists who care for immunocompromised children ensure appropriate immunizations are provided to these patients and their household and community contacts. We will review available data supporting the current guidelines regarding vaccine dosing, scheduling, and formulations to prevent influenza, COVID-19, and RSV infections in immunocompromised children. Furthermore, we highlight key knowledge gaps and areas of current investigation aimed at improving respiratory viral vaccine immunogenicity and optimize protection.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) alongside influenza and SARS-CoV-2, targeting immunocompromised pediatric patients (e.g., transplant recipients, chemotherapy recipients, and those on biologic modifiers) and their household contacts. The central question concerns vaccine recommendations, dosing, scheduling, and formulations to prevent severe RSV infections in this vulnerable population.
Evidence base and method
The review is a narrative synthesis of existing data, as no systematic search, database, or meta-analysis methodology is described. It extrapolates guidelines primarily from studies in healthy children and immunocompromised adults due to limited primary data in immunocompromised children.
Consensus and conclusions
Immunocompromised children face higher rates of hospitalization, ICU admission, and mortality from RSV due to immune dysfunction and environmental exposures. Current vaccine recommendations are based on extrapolated evidence, with consensus on the need for optimized protection.
Controversies and gaps
Key gaps include insufficient primary data on immunological responses and vaccine efficacy specifically in immunocompromised children. Suboptimal vaccination rates and vaccine hesitancy further complicate risk mitigation.
Research and practice implications
Priorities include generating target-specific efficacy data, improving vaccination coverage among immunocompromised children and contacts, and enhancing surveillance for RSV in this population. Clinicians must ensure timely immunizations.

内源性大麻素系统调节呼吸系统疾病:治疗视角。

Modulation of Respiratory Diseases by the Endocannabinoid System: A Therapeutic Perspective.
作者： Shiyuan Qin, Siyang Shen, Xiaoqin Chen, Pengfei Yi, Bendong Fu, Luyuan Peng
摘要中文翻译呼吸系统疾病是全球主要的健康负担,其特点是高 患病率 和显着的发病率和 死亡率。呼吸道直接且持续地暴露于外部环境,使其特别容易受到病原体、污染物和其他有害物质的侵害。这种暴露通常会引发过度的炎症反应,损害气血屏障的完整性,导致气体交换受损、缺氧和呼吸衰竭。因此,调节肺部炎症和增强屏障功能是关键的治疗目标。内源性大麻素系统是一种普遍存在的信号网络,由大麻素受体、内源性配体和代谢酶组成,已成为这些过程的关键调节剂。本综述总结了 ECS 在主要呼吸系统疾病中的作用,包括哮喘、肺纤维化、呼吸道合胞病毒 感染和急性肺损伤。证据强调了通过受体特异性配体和内源性大麻素降解酶抑制剂等策略靶向 ECS 的治疗潜力。然而,ECS 调制的上下文相关性质需要精确的干预。未来的努力应集中于开发选择性疗法并在临床环境中验证其疗效,将 ECS 定位为创新呼吸系统疾病管理的复杂目标。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述重点关注 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 感染作为一种主要呼吸道疾病,探讨调节内源性大麻素系统 (ECS) 解决肺部炎症和屏障功能障碍的治疗潜力。
证据基础与方法
该综述总结了 ECS 在主要呼吸道疾病(包括 RSV 感染)中的作用,但没有具体说明数据库、搜索日期或研究计数。
共识与结论
ECS 是呼吸过程的重要调节剂,通过受体特异性配体或酶抑制剂靶向它具有治疗 RSV 感染和其他呼吸系统疾病的潜力。
争议与缺口
ECS 调节的环境依赖性需要精确的干预,并且缺乏在临床环境中验证过的 RSV 选择性治疗方法。
科研与实践启示
未来的努力应集中于开发选择性 ECS 靶向疗法并验证其在临床环境中的功效,以改善 RSV 管理。

Modulation of Respiratory Diseases by the Endocannabinoid System: A Therapeutic Perspective.

Authors: Shiyuan Qin, Siyang Shen, Xiaoqin Chen, Pengfei Yi, Bendong Fu, Luyuan Peng
Original abstract (English)
Respiratory system diseases represent a major global health burden, characterized by high prevalence and significant morbidity and mortality. The respiratory tract's direct and continuous exposure to the external environment makes it particularly vulnerable to pathogens, pollutants and other injurious agents. This exposure often triggers excessive inflammatory responses and compromises the integrity of the air-blood barrier, leading to impaired gas exchange, hypoxia and respiratory failure. Therefore, modulating pulmonary inflammation and enhancing barrier function are critical therapeutic objectives. The endocannabinoid system, a ubiquitous signalling network comprising cannabinoid receptors, endogenous ligands and metabolic enzymes, has emerged as a crucial modulator of these processes. This review summarizes the role of the ECS in major respiratory diseases, including asthma, pulmonary fibrosis, respiratory syncytial virus infection and acute lung injury. The evidence highlights the therapeutic potential of targeting the ECS through strategies such as receptor-specific ligands and inhibitors of endocannabinoid-degrading enzymes. However, the context-dependent nature of ECS modulation necessitates precise intervention. Future efforts should focus on developing selective therapeutics and validating their efficacy in clinical settings, positioning the ECS as a sophisticated target for innovative respiratory disease management.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review focuses on respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) infection as a major respiratory disease, examining the therapeutic potential of modulating the endocannabinoid system (ECS) to address pulmonary inflammation and barrier dysfunction.
Evidence base and method
The review summarizes the role of the ECS in major respiratory diseases, including RSV infection, but does not specify databases, search dates, or study counts.
Consensus and conclusions
The ECS is a crucial modulator of respiratory processes, and targeting it through receptor-specific ligands or enzyme inhibitors holds therapeutic potential for RSV infection and other respiratory diseases.
Controversies and gaps
The context-dependent nature of ECS modulation necessitates precise intervention, and there is a lack of selective therapeutics validated in clinical settings for RSV.
Research and practice implications
Future efforts should focus on developing selective ECS-targeted therapeutics and validating their efficacy in clinical settings to improve RSV management.

呼吸道合胞病毒 - 大英百科全书

respiratory syncytial virus - Encyclopedia Britannica
新闻简报呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 是一种常见的传染性病原体,会导致易感人群(特别是六个月以下的婴儿、幼儿和老年人)出现轻度感冒样疾病,但会导致严重的下呼吸道疾病。它具有高度传染性,60-70% 的儿童在一岁时感染,几乎全部在两岁时感染。RSV 通过直接接触、空气传播和受污染的表面传播,症状包括流鼻涕、发烧、严重喘息和呼吸困难等。该病毒具有季节性,在温带地区的冬季达到高峰。治疗 的选择有限,利巴韦林作为主要药物治疗,单克隆抗体可用于高危婴儿的预防。预防强调卫生并避免 暴发 期间的密切接触。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 8 月 1 日。证据中未报告具体事件日期。
地点与对象
全球性,重点是温带地区。受影响的人群包括六个月以下的婴儿、幼儿和老年人。
事件
关于 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 的一般信息,呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 是一种传染性病原体,会导致易感人群出现轻度感冒样疾病,但会导致严重的下呼吸道疾病。没有报告 暴发 或事件。
规模、影响与风险
据估计,60-70% 的儿童在一岁时被感染,几乎全部在两岁时被感染。严重的 毛细支气管炎 和肺炎常见于六个月以下的婴儿、幼儿和老年人。RSV 具有季节性,在温带地区每年冬季流行。
应对、状态与不确定性
治疗 含有利巴韦林,可通过单克隆抗体对高危婴儿进行预防。预防措施包括洗手、清洁表面、避免密切接触和咳嗽时遮盖。来源是一篇完整的百科全书文章,没有未解决的信息。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

respiratory syncytial virus - Encyclopedia Britannica

News Brief
Respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) is a common infectious agent causing mild coldlike illness but severe lower respiratory disease in vulnerable populations, particularly infants under six months, young children, and the elderly. It is highly contagious, with 60-70% of children infected by age one and nearly all by age two. RSV spreads through direct contact, airborne transmission, and contaminated surfaces, with symptoms ranging from runny nose and fever to severe wheezing and breathing difficulties. The virus is seasonal, peaking in winter in temperate regions. Treatment options are limited, with ribavirin as the primary pharmacological therapy, and a monoclonal antibody available for prevention in high-risk infants. Prevention emphasizes hygiene and avoiding close contact during outbreaks.
Original source text (English)

Britannica Editors Last updated Aug. 1, 2026 •History Britannica AI Ask Anything Table of Contents Table of Contents Ask Anything respiratory syncytial virus (RSV), infectious agent of the respiratory system that typically produces a mild coldlike illness but in susceptible individuals, particularly infants under six months of age, is a major cause of severe and potentially fatal lower respiratory disease. Severe bronchiolitis and pneumonia caused by respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) occurs most commonly in the elderly and in young children. An estimated 60 to 70 percent of children have been infected with RSV by age one, and nearly all children have been infected by age two. Because RSV effectively evades immunity, repeat infections are common. RSV infection is seasonal, occurring as an annual epidemic during the winter in most countries in temperate regions. What's the Difference Between Bacteria and Viruses?Knowing the difference isn't just a technical distinction—it can save lives.(more) See all videos for this article Direct person-to-person contact is thought to be the most common route of RSV transmission. The virus also can be transmitted through the air, such as by coughing or sneezing, and through direct contact with a contaminated surface followed by touching of the face. Symptoms generally appear within four to six days of infection and include runny nose, cough, sore throat, headache, fever, and wheezing. In instances of severe illness, affected individuals may have a high fever, severe wheezing, rapid or laboured breathing, and bluish lips or fingers. Infants may only show signs of irritability, fatigue, and breathing difficulties, even in severe cases. Related Topics: pneumonia bronchiolitis ribavirin respiratory syncytial virus infection Pneumovirus (Show more) On the Web: National Center for Biotechnology Information - Respiratory Syncytial Virus Infection in Children (Aug. 01, 2026) (Show more) See all related content The only pharmacological therapy available for treatment of RSV is the nucleoside analogue ribavirin, which can be administered orally, parenterally (by injection or infusion), or by inhalation. An injectable humanized monoclonal antibody is available for prevention of severe RSV illness in high-risk infants and children. It provides passive immunity and must by given by intramuscular injection once a month during RSV season. Transmission can be prevented with frequent handwashing, cleaning of surfaces, avoiding close contact with others, and covering the mouth and nose with the upper arm when coughing or sneezing. 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV)

Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV)
新闻简报呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 仍然是一个重大的全球健康威胁,特别是影响婴儿和老年人。婴儿期早期 RSV 感染会增加儿童哮喘的风险,尤其是有过敏或哮喘家族史的儿童。对于老年人来说,RSV 会导致严重的后果和心脏并发症,疫苗 可以有效地将 60 岁及以上成年人的住院率减少 80% 以上。孕产妇 疫苗接种 和 尼塞韦单抗 在新生儿风险最高的月份提供补充保护。世界卫生组织发布了第一份关于婴儿 RSV 免疫的立场文件,强调需要全年获得免疫接种,以尽量减少季节性 暴发。尽管取得了这些进步,但仍需要采取额外的干预措施,因为仅 HEPA 净化器并不能完全减少教室的病毒暴露。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026-08-02。证据中没有报告具体的事件日期。
地点与对象
全球范围,特别关注婴儿、老年人以及早产儿、患有基础疾病的儿童、白血病患者等弱势群体。
事件
呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 是五岁以下儿童急性下呼吸道感染的主要原因,对全世界儿童健康构成持续威胁。婴儿早期感染 RSV 会显着增加患儿童哮喘的风险,尤其是有过敏或哮喘家族史的儿童。RSV 还会导致老年人出现严重后果和心脏并发症。
规模、影响与风险
RSV 导致成人住院率较高,丹麦的一项大型真实世界随机试验发现,RSVpreF 疫苗 将 60 岁及以上成人与 RSV 相关的住院率降低了 80% 以上。单剂量的 RSV 疫苗 可以为老年人提供 58% 的住院保护,但对于免疫功能低下的个体来说,效果有所不同。全年接种 RSV 疫苗可能会减少全国主要季节性 暴发 的发生。
应对、状态与不确定性
儿科专家正在为呼吸道病毒季节期间 RSV 病例的预期增加做好准备。孕产妇 疫苗接种 和 尼塞韦单抗 共同提供补充策略,在新生儿风险最高的月份提供保护。世界卫生组织 (WHO) 发布了第一份关于保护婴儿免受 RSV 感染的免疫产品的立场文件。然而,HEPA 净化器并不能完全减少教室病毒暴露,这表明需要采取额外的干预措施。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV)

News Brief
Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) remains a significant global health threat, particularly affecting infants and older adults. Early RSV infection in infancy increases the risk of childhood asthma, especially in children with a family history of allergy or asthma. For older adults, RSV causes severe outcomes and heart complications, with vaccines showing effectiveness in reducing hospitalizations by over 80% in adults aged 60 and over. Maternal vaccination and nirsevimab provide complementary protection for newborns during their highest-risk months. The WHO has issued its first position paper on RSV immunization for infants, emphasizing the need for year-round access to immunization to minimize seasonal outbreaks. Despite these advances, additional interventions are needed as HEPA purifiers alone do not fully reduce classroom viral exposure.
Original source text (English)
Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) Nirsevimab is a long-acting monoclonal antibody that provides season-long protection against RSV in infants, reducing medically attended infections and hospitalizations. Maternal vaccination and nirsevimab together offer complementary strategies to protect newborns during their highest-risk months. Location and population
Global, with specific focus on infants, older adults, and vulnerable populations such as premature babies, children with underlying diseases, and leukemia patients.
Event
Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) is a leading cause of acute lower respiratory infections in children under five and poses a persistent threat to child health worldwide. Early-infancy infection with RSV significantly increases the risk of developing childhood asthma, especially in children with a family history of allergy or asthma. RSV also causes severe outcomes and heart complications in older adults.
Scale, impact and risk
RSV leads to high hospitalization rates in adults, with a large real-world randomized trial in Denmark finding that the RSVpreF vaccine reduced RSV-related hospitalizations in adults aged 60 and over by over 80%. A single dose of the RSV vaccine offers 58% protection against hospitalization in older adults, but effectiveness varies in immunocompromised individuals. Pediatric experts are preparing for an expected rise in cases of RSV during the respiratory virus season. Maternal vaccination and nirsevimab together offer complementary strategies to protect newborns during their highest-risk months. The World Health Organization (WHO) has published its first-ever position paper on immunization products to protect infants against RSV. However, HEPA purifiers do not fully reduce classroom viral exposure, suggesting additional interventions are needed.

征求建议书:优化孕产妇 RSV 免疫接种(美国) -fundsforNGOs

Request for Proposals: Optimizing Maternal RSV Immunization Delivery (United States) - fundsforNGOs
新闻简报辉瑞全球医疗补助金 (GMG) 与母胎医学协会 (SMFM) 合作,推出了质量改进补助金机会,以加强美国医疗保健系统中孕产妇 RSV 免疫接种。该计划的资金高达 200,000 美元,支持基于证据的项目,以解决意识有限、工作流程挑战和健康差异等障碍。符合条件的组织可以在 2026 年 9 月 10 日之前申请从 2026 年 12 月开始的项目,整个 2027-2028 RSV 季节最长持续时间为 18 个月。该赠款优先考虑可扩展的模型,以改善 疫苗 的采用、医疗保健工作流程集成、提供者与患者的沟通以及孕产妇保健的公平性,旨在可持续地改进保护新生儿免受严重 RSV 疾病的侵害。

美国,针对医疗保健系统、提供者和服务不足的社区,包括农村人口和孕产妇护理沙漠。
事件
辉瑞全球医疗补助金 (GMG) 与母胎医学协会 (SMFM) 合作发起的质量改进补助金机会,旨在改善孕产妇妊娠期间 RSV 免疫接种。
规模、影响与风险
可用资金:单地点或区域项目最高可达 100,000 美元,大型多地点项目最高可达 200,000 美元,总资金约为 200,000 美元。该赠款旨在增加 疫苗 的覆盖范围、减少医疗保健差距并改善孕产妇和婴儿的健康结果。
应对、状态与不确定性
该计划的重点是应用现有证据和最佳实践来改进 疫苗接种 流程,而不是进行临床研究。受资助的项目应展示资助期间可衡量的进展和成果,重点关注系统层面的改进和可持续性。

Request for Proposals: Optimizing Maternal RSV Immunization Delivery (United States) - fundsforNGOs

News Brief
Pfizer Global Medical Grants (GMG), in partnership with the Society for Maternal-Fetal Medicine (SMFM), has launched a Quality Improvement Grant opportunity to enhance maternal RSV immunization delivery across U.S. healthcare systems. With funding up to USD 200,000, the initiative supports evidence-based projects addressing barriers such as limited awareness, workflow challenges, and health disparities. Eligible organizations can apply by September 10, 2026, for projects starting December 2026, with a maximum 18-month duration through the 2027–2028 RSV season. The grant prioritizes scalable models improving vaccine uptake, healthcare workflow integration, provider-patient communication, and equity in maternal healthcare, aiming to create sustainable improvements in protecting newborns from severe RSV illness.
Original source text (English)
Deadline: 10-Sep-2026 Pfizer Global Medical Grants (GMG), in collaboration with the Society for Maternal-Fetal Medicine (SMFM), has launched a Quality Improvement Grant opportunity to improve maternal RSV immunization delivery during pregnancy across healthcare systems in the United States. The grant supports evidence-based quality improvement projects that increase vaccine access, reduce barriers, improve healthcare workflows, and strengthen maternal and infant health outcomes. Overview of Pfizer Global Medical Grants Maternal RSV Immunization Quality Improvement Grant Pfizer Global Medical Grants (GMG), together with the Society for Maternal-Fetal Medicine (SMFM), is supporting independent quality improvement initiatives focused on improving the delivery of maternal Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) immunization during pregnancy. The grant opportunity aims to strengthen healthcare systems by supporting projects that improve how maternal RSV vaccination is delivered, accessed, and adopted in real-world clinical settings. The program focuses on applying existing evidence and best practices to improve vaccination processes rather than conducting clinical research or testing vaccine effectiveness. The initiative encourages healthcare organizations, providers, and community partners to develop scalable models that improve: - Maternal RSV immunization uptake. - Vaccine access. - Healthcare workflow integration. - Patient-provider communication. - Equity in maternal healthcare. - Long-term sustainability of immunization programs. Purpose of the Grant Program The primary goal of this Quality Improvement Grant is to support healthcare systems in improving maternal RSV immunization during pregnancy through practical and sustainable improvements. The program seeks to address challenges such as: - Limited awareness about maternal RSV immunization. - Healthcare workflow barriers. - Communication gaps between providers and patients. - Vaccine access challenges. - Health disparities affecting underserved communities. - Lack of coordination between maternal and pediatric healthcare services. Funded projects should focus on improving healthcare delivery systems and creating measurable improvements in vaccination outcomes. What Is Maternal RSV Immunization? Maternal RSV immunization refers to vaccination provided during pregnancy to help protect newborn infants from Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) infection. RSV is a common respiratory virus that can cause serious illness, especially in infants and young children. Improving maternal RSV immunization delivery can help: - Increase protection for newborns. - Reduce severe RSV-related illness. - Improve preventive healthcare practices. - Strengthen coordination between maternal and infant care providers. Key Focus Areas of the Grant The grant supports projects that improve maternal RSV immunization through system-level interventions and healthcare improvements. Priority areas include: Improving Healthcare Workflow Integration Projects may focus on integrating RSV immunization into routine maternal healthcare services. Examples include: - Adding vaccination processes into prenatal care workflows. - Improving electronic health record reminders. - Creating standardized vaccination protocols. - Reducing missed vaccination opportunities. Strengthening Care Coordination The program encourages collaboration between healthcare professionals involved in maternal and infant care. Potential partners include: - Maternal-fetal medicine specialists. - Obstetricians. - Nurses. - Midwives. - Doulas. - Pediatricians. - Family medicine providers. - Pharmacists. - Public health organizations. - Community organizations. Improving Provider and Patient Communication Projects may develop strategies to improve understanding and acceptance of maternal RSV vaccination. Activities may include: - Provider education. - Patient communication tools. - Vaccine counseling approaches. - Awareness initiatives. Reducing Barriers to Vaccine Uptake The grant supports initiatives that address structural and behavioral barriers, including: - Limited healthcare access. - Vaccine hesitancy. - Transportation challenges. - Healthcare inequalities. - Information gaps. Increasing Health Equity Priority is given to projects that improve vaccination access among: - Underserved communities. - Rural populations. - Maternal care deserts. - Communities experiencing healthcare disparities. Why This Grant Opportunity Matters Maternal RSV immunization plays an important role in protecting infants from serious respiratory illness. However, healthcare systems may face challenges in ensuring that eligible pregnant individuals receive recommended vaccinations. This grant supports improvements that can help healthcare organizations: - Increase vaccine coverage. - Improve maternal and infant health outcomes. - Create sustainable immunization systems. - Reduce healthcare disparities. - Develop scalable quality improvement models. By focusing on system-level improvements, the program aims to create lasting changes in maternal healthcare delivery. Funding Information The Pfizer Global Medical Grants Quality Improvement Grant provides financial support for eligible quality improvement initiatives. Available Funding - Single-site or regional projects: Up to USD 100,000 - Large multi-site projects: Up to USD 200,000 Estimated Total Funding Available - Approximately USD 200,000 Funding amounts may vary depending on project scope, impact, and available resources. Project Timeline Funded projects are expected to follow the proposed implementation schedule. Key timeline details include: - Expected project start date: December 2026 - Project period: Through the 2027–2028 RSV and respiratory disease season - Maximum project duration: 18 months Projects should demonstrate measurable progress and outcomes during the funding period. Who Is Eligible to Apply? The opportunity is open to organizations in the United States proposing independent quality improvement initiatives. Eligible applicants should: - Be involved in healthcare delivery or improvement activities. - Have the ability to implement maternal RSV immunization improvement projects. - Demonstrate organizational capacity. - Develop measurable quality improvement goals. Applicants may include healthcare organizations, healthcare systems, and collaborative groups working to improve maternal healthcare delivery. Eligible Project Characteristics Strong applications should include: - Evidence-based quality improvement strategies. - Clear implementation plans. - Measurable outcomes. - Sustainable healthcare system improvements. - Multidisciplinary collaboration. - Defined roles for participating organizations. Projects should focus on improving current healthcare practices rather than conducting clinical research. How to Apply for the Pfizer Maternal RSV Immunization Grant Applicants must submit applications through the designated online application portal. A complete application should include: Project Proposal Applicants should describe: - Project objectives. - Current challenges. - Proposed quality improvement strategies. - Expected impact. - Implementation approach. Project Timeline Applications should provide: - Key project activities. - Implementation milestones. - Expected completion dates. - Evaluation periods. Organizational Information Applicants must provide details about: - Organization background. - Relevant healthcare experience. - Project leadership team. - Partner organizations. Budget Information Applicants must submit: - Detailed project budget. - Requested funding amount. - Budget justification. - Costs in U.S. dollars. Supporting Documentation Applications should include required supporting documents listed in the official request for proposals. Allowable and Non-Allowable Expenses Funding may support project implementation activities. Allowable Expenses Grant funds may support: - Quality improvement activities. - Project implementation. - Program coordination. - Equipment hire or leasing when appropriate. - Other approved project-related expenses. Non-Allowable Expenses Funding cannot be used for: - Capital purchases. - Purchase of Pfizer therapeutic products. - Activities unrelated to quality improvement objectives. Institutional overhead is allowed according to Pfizer’s maximum allowable rate. Project Requirements Successful projects should demonstrate: Measurable Quality Improvement Outcomes Projects should track improvements such as: - Increased maternal RSV vaccination rates. - Improved healthcare access. - Better patient engagement. - Reduced vaccination barriers. Sustainable System Changes Projects should create improvements that continue beyond the grant period. Examples include: - Updated clinical workflows. - Improved provider training systems. - Long-term vaccination strategies. Multidisciplinary Collaboration Projects should involve appropriate partners with clearly defined responsibilities. Application Review Process Applications will be reviewed by an independent Expert Review Panel consisting of medical and quality improvement experts. The review process will consider: Evidence-Based Approach Projects should use proven strategies and available evidence to improve healthcare practices. Potential Clinical Impact Reviewers will assess the potential to improve: - Maternal care. - Infant health outcomes. - Vaccination coverage. Quality Improvement Design Applications should demonstrate: - Clear goals. - Effective implementation plans. - Measurable outcomes. Scalability and Sustainability Priority will be given to projects that can be expanded or adapted across healthcare settings. Funding Decision Process During review, applicants may be contacted for additional clarification or information. After evaluation: - Applicants will receive funding decisions by email. - Selected organizations must complete a written grant agreement with Pfizer. - Funding will be released after agreement completion. Common Mistakes to Avoid When Applying Proposing Clinical Research Instead of Quality Improvement The grant supports implementation and healthcare improvement projects, not clinical trials or vaccine efficacy research. Lack of Measurable Outcomes Applications should clearly explain how success will be measured. Weak Implementation Strategy Applicants should provide detailed plans showing how project activities will be carried out. Limited Collaboration Projects should include appropriate healthcare and community partners where relevant. Ignoring Health Equity Applications should consider barriers affecting underserved communities and maternal care access. Frequently Asked Questions (FAQ) What is the Pfizer Global Medical Grants Maternal RSV Immunization Grant? It is a quality improvement funding opportunity supporting healthcare initiatives that improve maternal RSV immunization delivery during pregnancy in the United States. Who can apply for this grant? Organizations in the United States proposing independent maternal RSV immunization quality improvement projects can apply. How much funding can applicants request? Single-site or regional projects may request up to USD 100,000, while larger multi-site projects may request up to USD 200,000. What types of projects are supported? The grant supports projects focused on workflow improvement, care coordination, provider communication, access improvement, vaccination uptake, and reducing healthcare barriers. Can grant funds be used for clinical research? No. The grant supports quality improvement initiatives and implementation strategies, not clinical research or therapeutic efficacy studies. When are funded projects expected to begin? Projects are expected to begin in December 2026 and continue through the 2027–2028 RSV and respiratory disease season. How are applications evaluated? Applications are reviewed by an independent expert panel based on evidence-based design, measurable outcomes, collaboration, feasibility, sustainability, and potential impact on clinical practice. Conclusion The Pfizer Global Medical Grants Quality Improvement Grant for Maternal RSV Immunization Delivery provides an opportunity for U.S.-based healthcare organizations to strengthen vaccination systems and improve maternal and infant health outcomes. By supporting evidence-based quality improvement projects, multidisciplinary partnerships, and sustainable healthcare solutions, the program aims to increase maternal RSV immunization access, reduce disparities, and improve protection for newborns across the United States. 石药集团(01093.HK)开发的重组呼吸道合胞病毒疫苗(SYS6057)在中国获临床试验批准 - 金融界
News Brief
Shiyang Group (01093.HK) has secured approval from China’s National Medical Products Administration to launch clinical trials for its recombinant respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) vaccine, SYS6057. The announcement, made on July 31, 2026, confirms that the vaccine has met regulatory requirements and can now be tested in human subjects within China. No outbreak data—such as cases, deaths, or exposures—are associated with this event; it represents a development milestone rather than a public health emergency. The approval enables the company to proceed with phase I/II studies, potentially accelerating the availability of an RSV prevention option for the Chinese population. No additional public health measures or risk assessments are required at this stage beyond the standard clinical trial oversight.

夹在两个 疫苗 时间表之间的孕妇现在可以在预约时携带婴儿住院号码 - Medical Daily

Pregnant Patients Caught Between Two Vaccine Schedules Now Have Infant Hospitalization Numbers to Bring to the Appointment - Medical Daily
新闻简报美国妇产科医师学会 (ACOG) 发布了独立于联邦建议的 2026 年孕产妇 疫苗接种 指南后,美国孕妇现在面临着相互冲突的免疫接种计划。为了帮助做出这一决定,新研究提供了有关婴儿结局的具体数据。匹兹堡大学和 UPMC 发表在《JAMA Network Open》上的一项研究发现,母亲 RSV 疫苗接种 与三个月以下婴儿因 RSV 相关呼吸道疾病住院治疗的有效性约为 68%,对于严重下呼吸道疾病的有效性为 69%。该研究调查了 2023-2024 年和 2024-2025 年 RSV 季节期间宾夕法尼亚州西部住院的婴儿。这些发现是在 CDC 进行的一项更大规模的分析中得出的,该分析证实了母亲 疫苗接种 和婴儿单克隆抗体对 RSV 住院治疗的保护作用,为医疗保健提供者和准父母提供了基于证据的指导。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026 年 7 月 31 日。事件日期:未明确报道,但该研究于 2026 年 6 月 5 日发布,涵盖 2023-2024 年和 2024-2025 年 RSV 季节。
地点与对象
研究人群:2023-2024 年和 2024-2025 年 RSV 季节期间,宾夕法尼亚州西部因呼吸道疾病住院的 90 天或更小的婴儿。
事件
已证实的研究结果:母体 RSV 疫苗接种 与婴儿因 RSV 相关呼吸道疾病住院的有效性约为 68% 相关,对于更严重的下呼吸道疾病的有效性约为 69%。由匹兹堡大学和 UPMC 的研究人员在 JAMA Network Open 上报告。
规模、影响与风险
有效率:RSV 相关呼吸道疾病住院治疗有效率 68%,严重下呼吸道疾病住院治疗有效率 69%。CDC 的另一项分析估计,在 2024-2025 年季节期间,母体 RSV 疫苗 和婴儿单克隆抗体可以保护婴儿免受 RSV 相关住院治疗。
应对、状态与不确定性
调查状态:研究结果已发表并得到证实。指导的不确定性:ACOG 在 6 月发布了自己的 2026 年孕产妇免疫计划,与联邦建议分开,导致对怀孕患者的指导产生了分歧。该研究提供了数据来指导这一决策过程。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Pregnant Patients Caught Between Two Vaccine Schedules Now Have Infant Hospitalization Numbers to Bring to the Appointment - Medical Daily

News Brief
Pregnant patients in the United States now face conflicting immunization schedules after the American College of Obstetricians and Gynecologists (ACOG) released its own 2026 maternal vaccination guidance separate from federal recommendations. To help navigate this decision, new research provides concrete data on infant outcomes. A University of Pittsburgh and UPMC study, published in JAMA Network Open, found that maternal RSV vaccination was associated with approximately 68% effectiveness against infant hospitalization for RSV-related respiratory illness and 69% effectiveness against severe lower respiratory tract disease among infants under three months old. The study examined infants hospitalized in western Pennsylvania during the 2023-2024 and 2024-2025 RSV seasons. These findings come amid a larger CDC analysis confirming the protective effects of both maternal vaccination and infant monoclonal antibodies against RSV hospitalization, offering evidence-based guidance for healthcare providers and expectant parents.

Conditions Pregnant Patients Caught Between Two Vaccine Schedules Now Have Infant Hospitalization Numbers to Bring to the Appointment Published Jul 31, 2026 3:15 PM EDT By Dorothy Brooks NORBERTO DUARTE | AFP via Getty Images Share on Twitter Share on Facebook Share on Pocket Pregnant patients in the United States are now navigating something that did not exist a year ago: two credible immunization schedules that do not match. And for the first time, they have hard numbers on infant hospitalizations to bring into that conversation. Researchers at the University of Pittsburgh and UPMC reported in JAMA Network Open on June 5 that among infants younger than three months, maternal RSV vaccination was associated with roughly 68 percent effectiveness against hospitalization for RSV-related respiratory illness and about 69 percent effectiveness against more severe lower respiratory tract disease. That finding lands in the middle of a guidance split. The American College of Obstetricians and Gynecologists released its own 2026 maternal immunization schedule in June, the first time the organization has published recommendations separate from federal ones. The schedule was endorsed by 13 medical and health organizations, including the American Academy of Pediatrics, the Infectious Diseases Society of America, and the Society for Maternal-Fetal Medicine, and it followed ACOG's withdrawal from the CDC's Advisory Committee on Immunization Practices in February 2026. For an expectant parent, this is not an abstract governance dispute. It is the reason two clinicians can give different answers in the same week, and the reason a pharmacy and an obstetric practice may operate from different documents. Numbers Parents Can Actually Use The Pittsburgh study was built around a question families actually ask. It examined infants 90 days old or younger who were hospitalized for respiratory illness in western Pennsylvania across the 2023-2024 and 2024-2025 RSV seasons, comparing outcomes between those whose mothers received the RSVpreF vaccine during pregnancy and those whose mothers did not. Infants who received the separate monoclonal antibody after birth were excluded so the two approaches would not be confused. "We designed this study to focus on what matters most to families," said lead author Anne-Marie Rick, MD, PhD, of Pitt School of Medicine and UPMC, in a UPMC statement. The scale of the underlying problem gives those percentages meaning. RSV is the leading cause of infant hospitalization in the United States, and CDC figures cited by the research team indicate roughly two to three of every 100 babies younger than three months are hospitalized each year because of it. Severe cases can require oxygen support or mechanical ventilation. A separate and larger analysis published in JAMA Pediatrics in March 2026, drawn from a seven-site CDC surveillance network, estimated that both the maternal RSV vaccine and the infant monoclonal antibody protected infants against RSV-associated hospitalization during the 2024-2025 season. That study was not industry-funded and reached a consistent conclusion, which strengthens the overall picture. Two Schedules, One Appointment The ACOG schedule lists four routinely recommended maternal vaccines: inactivated or recombinant influenza vaccine at any trimester, COVID-19 vaccine, Tdap preferably during weeks 27 through 36 of each pregnancy, and the maternal RSV vaccine between 32 weeks 0 days and 36 weeks 6 days of gestation. The clearest point of divergence is COVID-19. ACOG continues to recommend COVID-19 vaccination during pregnancy; current CDC guidance does not carry the same recommendation. ACOG also specifies that the live attenuated intranasal influenza vaccine is not indicated during pregnancy, though it is acceptable after delivery. ACOG has stated its position plainly. "It is well documented that respiratory conditions can cause poor outcomes during pregnancy," ACOG President Steven J. Fleischman, MD, MBA, said in an organizational statement. On RSV specifically, the two approaches are complementary rather than competing. Clinical guidance holds that an infant generally needs one form of protection, not both. If the mother received the vaccine at least 14 days before delivery, the infant typically does not need the monoclonal antibody. If she was not vaccinated, or delivered within 14 days of vaccination, the infant should receive the antibody after birth during RSV season. Timing is also seasonal. In most of the continental United States, the maternal RSV vaccine is given between September and January, which means patients due this fall are making the decision right now. Jurisdictions with different seasonality, including Alaska and some tropical territories, follow local guidance. Reading the Study's Limits Alongside Its Findings The funding and design details belong next to the headline number, not buried below it. The JAMA Network Open study was funded by Pfizer Inc through a collaboration with the University of Pittsburgh, and Pfizer employees are among the co-authors. Rick reported receiving grant support from Pfizer during the study and personal fees plus a site principal investigator role for a Pfizer vaccine trial outside the submitted work. Several other Pitt and UPMC co-authors reported Pfizer grant support. Pfizer manufactures the RSVpreF vaccine that the study evaluated. Beyond funding, the design carries real constraints. This was a retrospective case-control analysis using a test-negative design within a single health system in one region, not a randomized trial. Effectiveness estimates from one health system and one geography may not transfer cleanly to populations with different demographics, care patterns, or RSV circulation. The analysis covered infants 90 days or younger, so it does not speak to protection later in the first year. The research team acknowledged those limits and is continuing the work. The study is part of an ongoing four-year effort that will follow the 2025-2026 and 2026-2027 seasons and extend the analysis to infants up to 180 days old, which should address how long protection lasts. Preparing for the Obstetrician Conversation The purpose of this data is to make the appointment more productive, not to settle the decision in advance. Vaccination during pregnancy is a personal medical decision that belongs with a qualified clinician who knows the individual history. Patients can reasonably ask which schedule their practice is following and why, since the answer determines what they will be offered. They can ask about timing, particularly whether their due date and the 32 to 36 week RSV window line up, and what happens if delivery comes early. They can ask whether the infant monoclonal antibody is the better route in their situation, and confirm the 14-day threshold that determines which product the baby needs. Insurance is worth raising directly. When professional society recommendations diverge from federal ones, coverage can follow the federal schedule, and patients should confirm with their plan rather than assume. Patients covered by Medicaid or receiving care at a federally qualified health center should ask what is stocked on site, since availability varies by practice and pharmacy. Considerable uncertainty remains. Whether federal guidance will shift again, how insurers will handle the divergence through the coming respiratory season, and how long maternal antibody protection persists in infants are all unresolved. Updated CDC respiratory season guidance and the next round of surveillance data are expected before the fall season begins. MedicalDaily will track both. Here Is How to Find Out Which Schedule Applies Frequently Asked Questions Why are pregnant patients getting different vaccine advice? ACOG released its own 2026 maternal immunization schedule in June, the first time it has diverged from federal recommendations, after withdrawing from the CDC advisory committee in February 2026. What is the main difference between the schedules? ACOG continues to recommend COVID-19 vaccination during pregnancy. Current CDC guidance does not carry the same recommendation. How effective was the RSV vaccine in the new study? Among infants younger than three months, maternal vaccination was associated with approximately 68 percent effectiveness against RSV hospitalization and 69 percent against more severe lung illness. Who funded that research? Pfizer Inc funded the study through a collaboration with the University of Pittsburgh, and Pfizer employees co-authored it. Several academic authors reported Pfizer grant support. Does a baby need the antibody shot if the mother was vaccinated? Generally no, if the mother was vaccinated at least 14 days before delivery. If she was not vaccinated, or delivered within 14 days, the infant should receive the monoclonal antibody during RSV season. When during pregnancy is the RSV vaccine given? Between 32 weeks 0 days and 36 weeks 6 days of gestation, typically September through January in most of the continental United States. What should patients ask their obstetrician? Which schedule the practice follows, how timing lines up with the due date, whether the infant antibody is preferable in their case, and how their insurance plan is handling the guidance difference. B routine priorityPublic-health news

NHS confirms major vaccine rollout for older adults from September - AOL.co.uk

News Brief
NHS England and UKHSA have confirmed a major expansion of the RSV vaccination programme starting 1 September 2026, now including adults aged 65-74 with clinical at-risk conditions such as chronic respiratory disease or immunosuppression. Previously available only to those aged 75 and over or in care homes, the free vaccine (Abrysvo®) aims to reduce severe RSV illness risks. GPs will administer the one-dose vaccine, with a 100% offer expected for eligible groups. The rollout follows JCVI advice and allows co-administration with COVID-19 vaccines but not influenza. RSV, a common cause of colds, can lead to serious complications like pneumonia in vulnerable populations.
Original source text (English)
Claire Schofield Updated Tue, 4 August 2026 at 1:04 pm UTC 0 Adults aged between 65 and 74 years in certain clinical at risk groups are to be offered the RSV jab (Image: Getty) NHS England and the UK Health Security Agency (UKHSA) have confirmed a major vaccine rollout for older adults from September. The NHS Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) vaccination programme is being expanded to adults aged between 65 and 74 years in certain clinical at risk groups this year. The RSV vaccine has been routinely available to older adults as they turn 75 since September 2024, and was later extended to include all adults aged 80 and over, and all residents in care homes in April 2026. But NHS has confirmed a further expansion from next month with the eligibility criteria to include adults aged 65 to 74 years following advice from the Joint Committee on Vaccination and Immunisation (JCVI). NHS England and the UKHSA confirmed the change last month and said GPs will offer the vaccine to those who are eligible from September 1. RSV is a common cause of coughs and colds and while it usually gets better by itself, it can cause severe illness for babies and older adults over the age of 75. Those who are infected with RSV typically experience cold-like symptoms including a runny or blocked nose, a cough, sneezing, tiredness and a high temperature, but it can also lead to more serious infections like pneumonia or bronchiolitis. The RSV vaccination is available on the NHS for free to eligible groups, which can help reduce the risk of becoming seriously ill from the infection. You can currently get the jab if: you're 28 weeks pregnant or more you're aged 75 or over you live in a care home for older adults But from September 1, 2026, adults aged 65 to 74 and in either of the following clinical at risk groups will become eligible to get the RSV vaccine: 1. Chronic respiratory disease including those with: poorly controlled asthma chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD), including chronic bronchitis and emphysema bronchiectasis cystic fibrosis interstitial lung fibrosis pneumoconiosis bronchopulmonary dysplasia 2. Immunosuppression due to disease or treatment. Confirming the expansion in a letter on July 1, Caroline Temmink, director of vaccination at NHS England, and Dr Mary Ramsay CBE, director of public health programmes (including immunisation) at the UKHSA, said: "From 1 September 2026, the eligibility criteria will be expanded further to include adults aged 65 to 74 years in certain clinical at risk groups as advised by the Joint Committee on Vaccination and Immunisation (JCVI): chronic respiratory disease immunosuppression due to disease or treatment "Key points about the changes to the programme: the vaccine used in the programme will continue to be Abrysvo® (Pfizer pre-F vaccine) on a one-dose schedule. Adults who receive the vaccine from 1 September 2026 because they are aged 65 to 74 years old and in a clinical risk group should not be invited for a further dose of Abrysvo® when they become 75 years old RSV vaccines can be administered at any time and offers of vaccination should continue throughout the year. RSV vaccination should not be routinely scheduled with influenza vaccination but can be co-administered with the COVID-19 vaccine GP providers are expected to deliver a 100% offer to eligible groups, with call and recall delivered at the earliest opportunity where community pharmacies have been commissioned to support the older adults RSV programme, an updated enhanced service specification will be issued ahead of 1 September, and commissioned community pharmacies will be invited by regions to enter a contract variation."

孕妇和父母更喜欢母体 RSV 注射而不是婴儿抗体注射

Pregnant women, parents favor maternal RSV shot vs. infant antibody shot
新闻简报美国最近的一项调查显示,与婴儿抗体注射相比,孕妇和父母更喜欢母体 RSV 疫苗接种。由埃默里大学研究人员进行的这项研究发现,58.2%的孕妇和48.3%的父母更喜欢产妇疫苗接种。RSV 仍然是美国婴儿住院的主要原因,因此预防至关重要。然而,对 RSV 的认识差距和对抗体注射安全性的担忧仍然是主要障碍。该研究强调,强有力的医疗保健提供者建议可以显着提高接受率,35.8% 犹豫不决的孕妇和 27% 的父母可能会遵守此类指导。研究人员强调需要进行有针对性的教育,以解决信息差距并提高免疫覆盖率。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Pregnant women, parents favor maternal RSV shot vs. infant antibody shot

News Brief
A recent U.S. survey reveals that pregnant women and parents strongly prefer maternal RSV vaccination over infant antibody shots. Conducted by Emory University researchers, the study found 58.2% of pregnant women and 48.3% of parents favored maternal vaccination. RSV remains the leading cause of infant hospitalizations in the U.S., making prevention crucial. However, gaps in knowledge about RSV and concerns about antibody shot safety persist as major barriers. The study highlights that strong healthcare provider recommendations could significantly increase acceptance rates, with 35.8% of hesitant pregnant women and 27% of parents likely to comply with such guidance. Researchers emphasize the need for targeted education to address information gaps and improve immunization coverage.
Original source text (English)
Reproductive & Maternal Health By Andrew (Drew) Rhoades Fact checked by Mindy Valcarcel, MS Purpose-built for physicians Learn more Read more Pregnant women, parents favor maternal RSV shot vs. infant antibody shot August 04, 2026 2 min read By Andrew (Drew) Rhoades Fact checked by Mindy Valcarcel, MS Add topic to email alerts Receive an email when new articles are posted on Please provide your email address to receive an email when new articles are posted on . If you continue to have this issue please contact customerservice@slackinc.com . Key takeaways: More than half of pregnant women and nearly half of parents preferred maternal RSV vaccination. Reasons for hesitancy include insufficient knowledge of RSV, and short- and long-term vaccine effects. More pregnant women and parents preferred maternal RSV vaccination over monoclonal antibody injections for young children, data show. Perspective from Margot Savoy, MD, MPH, FAAFP According to Rachael Porter, MPH, a doctoral student at Emory University Rollins School of Public Health, and colleagues, RSV is the leading cause of hospitalization among children in the United States, “with the highest age-specific hospitalizations occurring in infants at 1 month, emphasizing the need for RSV infection protection of infants as soon as possible after birth.” More than half of pregnant women and nearly half of parents preferred maternal RSV vaccination. Image: Adobe Stock The maternal RSV vaccine (Abrysvo, Pfizer) and antibody shots nirsevimab (Beyfortus, AstraZeneca and Sanofi) and clesrovimab (Enflonsia, Merck) reduce the risk for RSV-associated hospitalizations in youth, the researchers noted in JAMA Network Open . However, there are gaps in immunization coverage, making it “necessary to design strategies that support families in their RSV immunization decisions,” Porter and colleagues wrote. In the analysis, the researchers conducted two concurrent national cross-sectional surveys consisting of 174 pregnant women and 1,765 parents with children aged 5 years and younger. “The survey included questions about infant RSV immunization preferences, RSV immunization intention or behaviors for the child, and maternal RSV vaccination intention or behaviors during pregnancy,” they wrote. Porter and colleagues found there were more pregnant women (58.2%; 95% CI, 50.9%-65.6%) and parents (48.3%; 95% CI, 46%-50.6%) who preferred maternal vaccination vs. pregnant women (6.2%; 95% CI, 2.6%-9.8%) and parents (9.4%; 95% CI, 8.1%-10.8%) who preferred the antibody shot. Of pregnant women eligible for vaccination, 55.5% (95% CI, 47.7%-63.3%) with positive intentions of immunization preferred the maternal RSV shot, the researchers wrote, whereas 35.8% (95% CI, 24.3%-47.3%) with negative or unsure intentions “would probably or definitely accept” maternal RSV vaccination following a strong recommendation from their health care provider. Meanwhile, 59.8% (95% CI, 57.5%-62.1%) of parents with positive intentions about immunization showed preference toward maternal RSV vaccination, and 27% (95% CI, 23.7%-30.2%) of parents with negative or unsure intentions would likely or definitely accept the maternal RSV shot if their HCP strongly recommended it. The top reasons for negative or uncertain attitudes toward immunization included a lack of knowledge about RSV or the RSV antibody shot (53.9%; 95% CI, 42.8%-65%) and concerns about the long-term (41.4%; 95% CI, 30.4%-52.4%) and short-term (23.8%; 95% CI, 14.3%-33.3%) safety of the antibody shot in youth. “Healthcare practitioner recommendation or other information sources may not sway the small group of pregnant women and parents who firmly state they do not want RSV immunization for themselves or their infants,” Porter and colleagues wrote, adding that the data show a need “for additional educational strategies to improve knowledge and awareness around the specifics of RSV prevention products.” “As these products are relatively new, issues of availability, accessibility, acceptability and knowledge among the eligible community will continue to impact uptake, as seen through varying coverage estimates reported,” they wrote. Perspective Back to Top Margot Savoy, MD, MPH, FAAFP Margot Savoy, MD, MPH, FAAFP The findings of this study tell us that interest in RSV prevention for pregnant patients and babies is there, but uncertainty is still present. Information gaps are the most common barrier — patients feeling that they don’t have enough information about the vaccine or antibody to make an informed decision. It’s notable that a percentage of patients with negative or unsure intentions said they would likely accept protection if strongly recommended by a healthcare professional; 35.8% for the maternal vaccine and 27.4% for the infant antibody. These are patients who can be moved with clear, trusted guidance. The findings also highlight the importance of patient education to improve knowledge about RSV and the proven benefits of maternal immunization and infant antibodies. Regarding patients preferring maternal vaccination to the infant antibody, vaccination during pregnancy has decades of precedent with Tdap, influenza, etc., so patients understand the concept. The antibody shot is a different kind of product, and families often don’t know how it works or why it’s needed. It’s less familiar, and this is another opportunity for family physicians and other clinicians to fill in the gaps and make sure patients have all the information they need to make an informed decision for themselves and their baby. A strong recommendation still moves a substantial number of patients who are unsure about the maternal RSV vaccine and antibodies. Even if only 27% to 36% of hesitant patients change their minds, that’s clinically significant. Those are infants who avoid RSV hospitalization because a clinician took the time to recommend vaccination or the antibody clearly, with both empathy and evidence. Counseling should be simple, direct and focused on what matters: RSV is common, dangerous for infants and preventable. When patients are worried about short- and long-term effects, clinicians should reassure them that these products were rigorously studied, are monitored continuously, and are recommended because the benefits significantly outweigh any potential risk. Maternal vaccination should be presented as routine prenatal care. When clinicians frame maternal RSV vaccination alongside Tdap and influenza, patients see it as standard newborn protection, not an optional add-on. Hesitancy often is not refusal, it’s uncertainty. The study shows that many hesitant patients are simply missing key information. That’s an opportunity. Also, consistency across the care team matters. When OB/GYNs, family physicians, pediatricians, nurses and pharmacists all deliver the same message, patients feel more confident. When it comes to addressing patient uncertainty, even small gains matter. If one-third of hesitant families accept vaccination after a strong recommendation, that’s thousands of infants protected nationwide, and that’s progress. Margot Savoy, MD, MPH, FAAFP American Academy of Family Physicians Disclosures: Savoy reports being employed by AAFP and serving as an author for the Merck Manuel chapter on immunizations.
